11	## 0. TL;DR
12	A coach or parent in Bangalore should land on KhelSutra, feel at home, set up their camera with confidence, and get highlights — **in Kannada or Hindi**, with **zero hand-holding**. This project isolates that goal into one tracked workstream-pair: **(A)** make the UI super friendly for non-tech-savvy users, and **(B)** localize to Hindi + Kannada. Both surfaces (`web/` React SPA + `site/` vanilla marketing) have **zero i18n today** `[verified]`, so the foundation — shared `locales/{en,kn,hi}` catalogs + a negotiation contract + CI parity — is the real deliverable; translations follow. **The single most important caveat:** translations are **human-reviewed** (Gemini drafts are *inference only, never training* — the standing guardrail), and Kannada/Hindi rendering is **blocked on the OFL font decision (D-FONT)**. The cheapest unblocked high-impact first slice is the **SPA i18n scaffold + language switcher (en-only)** — it locks the shared catalog/negotiation contract and adds the render-test safety net the SPA currently lacks.
13	
14	## 1. Problem & goal
15	**Why now.** The public site (khelsutra.guru) is live, clean, honest, and tested. The PMF beachhead is **Bangalore badminton academies**; many coaches, parents, and players are far more comfortable in **Kannada** than English, and most are **not highly tech-savvy** `[verified — design intent]` (`UI_PROPOSAL.md:25,28`, `I18N_PLAN.md:10-13`, `PMF_MARKET_RESEARCH.md:116`, `KHELSUTRA_PRODUCT_OVERVIEW.md:13/50/61`). An English-only, knob-heavy product adds friction at the exact point of first contact.
16	
17	**Concrete outcome ("good" looks like):** a Kannada/Hindi-comfortable, phone-first, non-technical coach/parent can — without help — (1) understand what the product does and how to record reliably, (2) request access / use the console, (3) pick rallies and get a reel, and (4) share it on WhatsApp, **entirely in their language**.

1	// Error helpers shared by the ingest flow and the build flow. Kept i18n-FREE: callers map an
2	// IngestErrorCode to a localized string (so hooks stay testable without a translation context),
3	// while the engine's own `detail` text passes through verbatim (it's the most specific message).
4	import { ApiError } from "../api/client";
5	
6	/** Pull FastAPI's {"detail": "..."} message out of an error body, else return the raw body. */
7	export function parseDetail(body: string): string {
8	  try {
9	    const j = JSON.parse(body) as { detail?: unknown };
10	    if (j && typeof j.detail === "string") return j.detail;
11	  } catch {
12	    /* not JSON — fall through to the raw body */
13	  }
14	  return body;
15	}
16	
17	/** Stable, localizable failure categories surfaced by the ingest flow.
18	 *  `engine` carries the engine's own message in `detail`; the rest are localized by code. */
19	export type IngestErrorCode = "invalidUri" | "networkError" | "emptyResult" | "timeout" | "engine";
20	
21	export interface IngestError {
22	  code: IngestErrorCode;
23	  /** Present for `engine` — the verbatim FastAPI detail / status; rendered as-is, not translated. */
24	  detail?: string;
25	}
26	
27	/** Classify a thrown client error into a stable code. A network throw (ApiError with no `status`)
28	 *  is the common "engine unreachable" case → its own friendly code; any HTTP error surfaces the
29	 *  engine's `detail` (e.g. an unsupported-scheme 400, a not-found 404). */
30	export function classifyError(err: unknown): IngestError {
31	  if (err instanceof ApiError) {
32	    if (err.status === undefined) return { code: "networkError" }; // fetch threw — engine truly unreachable
33	    if (err.body) {
34	      const detail = parseDetail(err.body);
35	      if (detail) return { code: "engine", detail }; // a real engine message (e.g. a 400 unsupported-scheme)
36	    }
37	    // A bare 5xx with no body is almost always the engine being down / a gateway that can't reach it
38	    // (e.g. the dev proxy when :8000 is off) — for a non-technical user a friendly "is it running?"
39	    // beats a cryptic "HTTP 500". A 4xx with no body keeps the status (it's a client-side mistake).
40	    if (err.status >= 500) return { code: "networkError" };
41	    return { code: "engine", detail: `HTTP ${err.status}` };
42	  }
43	  return { code: "engine", detail: String(err) };
44	}
45	
[/external_agent_tool_result]

19	export function RallyGrid({ rallies, isSelected, onToggle, ordinalOf }: Props) {

36	              aria-pressed={selected}
37	              aria-label={t(selected ? "grid.inReel" : "grid.notInReel", { n })}
38	              className={

44	            >

48	                  aria-hidden="true"
49	                  className={
50	                    "inline-flex h-5 w-5 items-center justify-center rounded-full text-xs " +
51	                    (selected ? "bg-green text-[#04140D]" : "border border-black/20 text-transparent")
52	                  }
53	                >
54	                  ✓

56	              </div>

1	import { useTranslation } from "react-i18next";
2	import { SUPPORTED_LOCALES, DEFAULT_LOCALE, type Locale } from "../i18n";
3	
4	// Each locale's label is its own autonym, shown in its own script. Kannada renders crisply once the
5	// bundled Noto Sans Kannada font lands (tracker B4); most devices already ship a system Kannada font.
6	const LANG_LABEL_KEY: Record<Locale, string> = {
7	  en: "lang.en",
8	  kn: "lang.kn",
9	  hi: "lang.hi",
10	};
11	
12	/**
13	 * A persistent language switcher. Selecting a locale calls i18next's changeLanguage, which the
14	 * detector caches to localStorage (LANG_STORAGE_KEY) — so the choice is the top-priority signal on
15	 * the next visit (negotiation: persisted pref wins). Icon-free + non-color active state via
16	 * aria-pressed keeps it usable for non-tech-savvy users (FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL §4).
17	 */
18	export function LanguageSwitcher() {
19	  const { t, i18n } = useTranslation();
20	  // Highlight the user's CHOSEN locale (i18n.language), not the resolved-for-content one:
21	  // resolvedLanguage falls back to `en` when a catalog is missing, so picking Kannada (content
22	  // still English until the kn catalog lands, B5) must still show Kannada as selected. Compare on
23	  // the base subtag so a detected "en-IN" highlights "en".
24	  const active = (i18n.language || DEFAULT_LOCALE).split("-")[0];
25	
26	  return (
27	    <nav aria-label={t("lang.label")} className="flex items-center gap-1">
28	      {SUPPORTED_LOCALES.map((loc) => {
29	        const isActive = active === loc;
30	        return (
31	          <button
32	            key={loc}
33	            type="button"
34	            lang={loc}
35	            aria-pressed={isActive}
36	            onClick={() => void i18n.changeLanguage(loc)}
37	            className={[
38	              "rounded-full px-3 py-1.5 text-sm font-semibold transition",
39	              isActive ? "bg-white text-ink" : "text-white/70 hover:text-white",
40	            ].join(" ")}
41	          >
42	            {t(LANG_LABEL_KEY[loc])}
43	          </button>
44	        );
45	      })}
46	    </nav>
47	  );
48	}
49	
[/external_agent_tool_result]

1	// i18n scaffold (tracker B1) — the shared localization foundation for KhelSutra Studio.
2	//
3	// Per docs/FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md + the engine's I18N_PLAN.md:
4	//   - keys, never prose (t("app.title"), enforced later by a no-bare-strings lint, B2);
5	//   - ICU MessageFormat for interpolation/plurals (Kannada/Hindi plural rules live in the catalog,
6	//     not the code — replaces the SPA's hand-rolled `count > 1 ? "s" : ""`);
7	//   - one negotiation order shared with the marketing site (B3);
8	//   - always-fallback to `en` so a missing translation degrades to English, never a raw key.
9	import i18n from "i18next";
10	import { initReactI18next } from "react-i18next";
11	import LanguageDetector from "i18next-browser-languagedetector";
12	import ICU from "i18next-icu";
13	import enCommon from "./locales/en/common.json";
14	import knCommon from "./locales/kn/common.json";
15	import hiCommon from "./locales/hi/common.json";
16	
17	// Supported locales (D6): `en` + `kn` + `hi`. Anything else falls back to `en`.
18	export const SUPPORTED_LOCALES = ["en", "kn", "hi"] as const;
19	export type Locale = (typeof SUPPORTED_LOCALES)[number];
20	export const DEFAULT_LOCALE: Locale = "en";
21	
22	// ONE persisted-preference key, shared with the marketing site's t() shim (D9 / tracker B3), so a
23	// visitor's language choice carries across both surfaces.
24	export const LANG_STORAGE_KEY = "khelsutra.lang";
25	
26	// Catalogs are co-located here (D9). `kn` + `hi` shipped (B5/B6, AI-drafted → native review
27	// pending). A missing key in any locale still falls back to `en` — the by-design invariant.
28	const resources = {
29	  en: { common: enCommon },
30	  kn: { common: knCommon },
31	  hi: { common: hiCommon },
32	} as const;
33	
34	void i18n
35	  .use(ICU)
36	  .use(LanguageDetector)
37	  .use(initReactI18next)
38	  .init({
39	    resources,
40	    fallbackLng: DEFAULT_LOCALE,
41	    supportedLngs: [...SUPPORTED_LOCALES],
42	    nonExplicitSupportedLngs: true, // en-IN -> en
43	    defaultNS: "common",
44	    ns: ["common"],
45	    interpolation: { escapeValue: false }, // React already escapes
46	    returnNull: false,
47	    // Negotiation order (I18N_PLAN §2.3): persisted pref -> ?lang= -> browser -> en.
48	    detection: {
49	      order: ["localStorage", "querystring", "navigator"],
50	      lookupLocalStorage: LANG_STORAGE_KEY,
51	      lookupQuerystring: "lang",
52	      caches: ["localStorage"],
53	    },
54	    react: { useSuspense: false },
55	  });
56	
57	// Keep <html lang> in sync with the active locale (screen readers + SEO use it). Reflects the
58	// resolved content language, so an `en` fallback for an absent locale still reads as `en`.
59	function syncDocumentLang(lng: string) {
60	  if (typeof document !== "undefined") {
61	    document.documentElement.lang = (i18n.resolvedLanguage || lng || DEFAULT_LOCALE).split("-")[0];
62	  }
63	}
64	i18n.on("languageChanged", syncDocumentLang);
65	syncDocumentLang(i18n.language ?? DEFAULT_LOCALE);
66	
67	export default i18n;
68

1	// CI guardrails that keep i18n "by design" (FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md §4):
2	//   1. KEY-PARITY — every locale catalog has EXACTLY the `en` key set (the extensibility proof:
3	//      drop a stub locale and this lists every gap). Trivially green while only `en` exists; it
4	//      starts enforcing the moment a kn/hi catalog lands (B5/B6).
5	//   2. NO-BARE-STRINGS (attribute level, v1) — user-facing component attributes (placeholder /
6	//      aria-label / title) must route through t(), never a hardcoded literal. A curated start per
7	//      the doc; an AST-level sweep of JSX text can extend it later.
8	import { describe, it, expect } from "vitest";
9	import { readdirSync, readFileSync } from "node:fs";
10	import { join } from "node:path";
11	
12	const LOCALES_DIR = join(import.meta.dirname, "locales");
13	const SRC_DIR = join(import.meta.dirname, "..");
14	
15	function flatten(obj: Record<string, unknown>, prefix = ""): string[] {
16	  const keys: string[] = [];
17	  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(obj)) {
18	    const key = prefix ? `${prefix}.${k}` : k;
19	    if (v && typeof v === "object" && !Array.isArray(v)) keys.push(...flatten(v as Record<string, unknown>, key));
20	    else keys.push(key);
21	  }
22	  return keys.sort();
23	}
24	
25	const loadKeys = (locale: string): string[] =>
26	  flatten(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(LOCALES_DIR, locale, "common.json"), "utf8")) as Record<string, unknown>);
27	
28	describe("i18n key-parity — every locale has EXACTLY the en keys", () => {
29	  const enKeys = loadKeys("en");
30	  const others = readdirSync(LOCALES_DIR, { withFileTypes: true })
31	    .filter((d) => d.isDirectory() && d.name !== "en")
32	    .map((d) => d.name);
33	
34	  it("en is the non-empty source catalog", () => {
35	    expect(enKeys.length).toBeGreaterThan(20);
36	  });
37	
38	  for (const loc of others) {
39	    it(`${loc} matches en exactly (no missing, no extra keys)`, () => {
40	      const keys = loadKeys(loc);
41	      const missing = enKeys.filter((k) => !keys.includes(k));
42	      const extra = keys.filter((k) => !enKeys.includes(k));
43	      expect({ locale: loc, missing, extra }).toEqual({ locale: loc, missing: [], extra: [] });
44	    });
45	  }
46	});
47	
48	describe("no-bare-strings — user-facing component attributes go through t()", () => {
49	  const FILES = [
50	    "App.tsx",
51	    "components/QueryBar.tsx",
52	    "components/RallyGrid.tsx",
53	    "components/SelectionFooter.tsx",
54	    "components/CompileResult.tsx",
55	    "components/LanguageSwitcher.tsx",
56	    "components/IngestPanel.tsx",
57	  ];
58	  // A hardcoded placeholder / aria-label / title with letters is a bare-string regression — it
59	  // must be an expression ({t(...)}), not a quoted literal.
60	  const BARE_ATTR = /\b(?:placeholder|aria-label|title)\s*=\s*"[^"]*[A-Za-z]{2}[^"]*"/g;
61	
62	  for (const f of FILES) {
63	    it(`${f}: no hardcoded placeholder/aria-label/title`, () => {
64	      const src = readFileSync(join(SRC_DIR, f), "utf8");
65	      expect(src.match(BARE_ATTR) ?? []).toEqual([]);
66	    });
67	  }
68	});
69	
70	describe("Indic fonts (B4) — self-hosted OFL Noto, no runtime CDN", () => {
71	  it("main.tsx bundles the OFL Noto Kannada + Devanagari fonts (self-hosted, not Google Fonts)", () => {
72	    const main = readFileSync(join(SRC_DIR, "main.tsx"), "utf8");
73	    expect(main).toMatch(/@fontsource-variable\/noto-sans-kannada/);
74	    expect(main).toMatch(/@fontsource-variable\/noto-sans-devanagari/);
75	  });
76	  it("the font stack falls back to Noto for Kannada + Devanagari glyphs", () => {
77	    const css = readFileSync(join(SRC_DIR, "index.css"), "utf8");
78	    expect(css).toMatch(/Noto Sans Kannada Variable/);
79	    expect(css).toMatch(/Noto Sans Devanagari Variable/);
80	  });
81	});

1	// Deterministic, dependency-free query compiler for the rally picker (PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md
2	// D2/D5). NO LLM, NO network — a tiny regex grammar over the ONLY honest, queryable axes the
3	// engine returns today: rally LENGTH (duration), ORDER (start_time / duration / shot_count),
4	// COUNT (top/first N), and SHOT COUNT (metadata.shot_count).
5	//
6	// Everything else a user might type — shot TYPE (smash/clear/drop…), PLAYER, SCORE — has no
7	// detector and no column, so we FLAG it as "not available yet" and NEVER fabricate a result.
8	// Parsing is pure and unit-tested; the QueryBar React component is a thin shell over it.
9	import type { RallySegment } from "../api/types";
10	
11	export type CompareOp = ">" | ">=" | "<" | "<=" | "=";
12	export type FilterField = "duration" | "shot_count";
13	/** One ANDed predicate over a rally field (e.g. duration > 10s). */
14	export interface Filter {
15	  field: FilterField;
16	  op: CompareOp;
17	  value: number; // seconds for duration; a raw count for shot_count
18	}
19	
20	export type SortKey = "duration" | "start_time" | "shot_count";
21	export interface Sort {
22	  key: SortKey;
23	  dir: "asc" | "desc";
24	}
25	
26	/** A recognized-but-unsupported concept — surfaced as a greyed "not available yet" hint. */
27	export type UnavailableKind = "shot-type" | "player" | "score";
28	export interface Unavailable {
29	  token: string;
30	  kind: UnavailableKind;
31	}
32	
33	export interface ParsedQuery {
34	  raw: string;
35	  /** ANDed together. */
36	  filters: Filter[];
37	  sort?: Sort;
38	  /** "top N" / "first N" / "N longest". */
39	  limit?: number;
40	  /** Concepts we understood but cannot honour (shot-type / player / score). */
41	  unavailable: Unavailable[];
42	  /** True when at least one actionable clause (filter | sort | limit) was understood. */
43	  recognized: boolean;
44	}
45	
46	export interface QueryResult {
47	  /** ALL rallies in display order (sorted if a sort was given, else input order). */
48	  ordered: RallySegment[];
49	  /** Ids matching the filters (then limited), in display order. */
50	  selectedIds: number[];
51	}
52	
53	// ── grammar fragments ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
54	// `(?!\d)` stops a greedy digit-run from backtracking and leaving stray trailing digits
55	// (so "over 10 smashes" can't degrade to "over 1" + "0 smashes").
56	const NUM = "(\\d+(?:\\.\\d+)?)(?!\\d)";
57	// Time / "shots" unit tokens. "shots?" is FIRST so it isn't eaten by the bare "s"; "mins?"
58	// already covers "min" (no redundant bare "min"); \b stops "m" matching "minutes" etc.
59	const UNIT_INNER = "shots?|seconds?|secs?|minutes?|mins?|m|s";
60	const UNIT = `(?:\\s*(${UNIT_INNER})\\b)?`;
61	
62	// Shot-TYPE / player / score nouns — concepts with NO detector and NO column. A number trailing
63	// one of these must NEVER become a real filter (honesty: never reroute an unsupported concept
64	// onto the duration axis — PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md §3/§4.3). These stems are shared with the
65	// "not available yet" flagging below so the two can never drift apart.
66	const SHOT_TYPE = "smash(?:es)?|clears?|drops?|dropshots?|net ?shots?|drives?|lifts?|serves?|kills?|taps?|pushes?|blocks?|slices?";
67	const PLAYER = "players?|opponents?|servers?|receivers?";
68	const SCORE = "points?|score|deuce|game point|set point|match point";
69	const UNAVAIL_WORD = `(?:${SHOT_TYPE}|${PLAYER}|${SCORE})`;
70	// Voids a comparator clause whose number is immediately followed by an unsupported noun.
71	const NOT_UNAVAIL = `(?!\\s*${UNAVAIL_WORD}\\b)`;
72	
73	interface OpPattern {
74	  op: CompareOp;
75	  re: RegExp;
76	  /** Which capture group holds the number / the unit. */
77	  numAt: number;
78	  unitAt: number;
79	}
80	
81	// Leading comparator ("over 10s"). The "no more than" / "at least" guards come BEFORE their
82	// bare counterparts ("more than" / "less than") so the negated phrase is consumed first.

1	// Vitest setup (runs before each test file's imports). DOM-env only: jsdom under Node 26 does not
2	// expose a Web Storage `localStorage`, which the i18n language detector and the persistence test
3	// both need. Node-env test files (typeof window === "undefined") skip this entirely.
4	if (typeof window !== "undefined" && typeof globalThis.localStorage === "undefined") {
5	  let store: Record<string, string> = {};
6	  const mem: Storage = {
7	    get length() {
8	      return Object.keys(store).length;
9	    },
10	    clear() {
11	      store = {};
12	    },
13	    getItem(key: string) {
14	      return Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(store, key) ? store[key] : null;
15	    },
16	    key(index: number) {
17	      return Object.keys(store)[index] ?? null;
18	    },
19	    removeItem(key: string) {
20	      delete store[key];
21	    },
22	    setItem(key: string, value: string) {
23	      store[key] = String(value);
24	    },
25	  };
26	  Object.defineProperty(window, "localStorage", { value: mem, configurable: true, writable: true });
27	  Object.defineProperty(globalThis, "localStorage", { value: mem, configurable: true, writable: true });
28	}
29	
[/external_agent_tool_result]

1	// @vitest-environment jsdom
2	//
3	// The ingest screen end to end: type a URI → submit → (mocked engine) → onLoaded gets the video_id;
4	// and the two failure surfaces a non-technical user actually hits — the engine's own 400 detail,
5	// and a friendly "is the engine running?" when it's unreachable. Mounts the real i18n catalog.
6	import { describe, it, expect, vi, beforeEach, afterEach } from "vitest";
7	import { render, screen, fireEvent, cleanup, act } from "@testing-library/react";
8	import i18n from "../i18n/index";
9	import { IngestPanel } from "./IngestPanel";
10	import type { JobStatus } from "../api/types";
11	
12	type FetchFn = typeof fetch;
13	
14	function httpOk(status: number, json: unknown) {
15	  return { ok: status >= 200 && status < 300, status, json: async () => json, text: async () => JSON.stringify(json) };
16	}
17	
18	function fetchSeq(process: unknown, jobs: JobStatus[]): FetchFn {
19	  let i = 0;
20	  return vi.fn(async (url: string | URL, init?: RequestInit) => {
21	    const u = String(url);
22	    if (u === "/api/process" && (init?.method ?? "GET") === "POST") return process;
23	    if (u.startsWith("/api/jobs/")) {
24	      const body = jobs[Math.min(i, jobs.length - 1)];
25	      i += 1;
26	      return httpOk(200, body);
27	    }
28	    throw new Error(`unexpected fetch ${u}`);
29	  }) as unknown as FetchFn;
30	}
31	
32	beforeEach(async () => {
33	  window.localStorage.clear();
34	  await i18n.changeLanguage("en"); // guarantees init completed + a known locale for the assertions
35	});
36	
37	afterEach(() => {
38	  cleanup();
39	  vi.restoreAllMocks();
40	  vi.unstubAllGlobals();

2	// UI + engine behind one origin; in dev Vite proxies `/api` → :8000). Every call carries a
3	// correlation id (header + logs) so a journey is traceable end to end.
4	import { log, newCorrelationId } from "../lib/log";

7	const BASE = "/api";
8	
9	export class ApiError extends Error {
10	  readonly cid?: string;
11	  readonly status?: number;
12	  readonly body?: string;
13	  constructor(message: string, opts: { cid?: string; status?: number; body?: string; cause?: unknown } = {}) {
14	    super(message);
15	    this.name = "ApiError";
16	    this.cid = opts.cid;
17	    this.status = opts.status;
18	    this.body = opts.body;
19	    if (opts.cause !== undefined) (this as { cause?: unknown }).cause = opts.cause;
20	  }
21	}
22	
23	interface RequestOpts {
24	  method?: string;
25	  body?: string;
26	  headers?: Record<string, string>;
27	  /** Correlation id to thread through this call; auto-generated if absent. */
28	  cid?: string;
29	}
30	
31	async function request<T>(path: string, opts: RequestOpts = {}): Promise<T> {
32	  const cid = opts.cid ?? newCorrelationId();
33	  const url = `${BASE}${path}`;
34	  const method = opts.method ?? "GET";
35	  log("info", "api.request", { cid, method, url });
36	
37	  let res: Response;
38	  try {
39	    res = await fetch(url, {
40	      method,
41	      body: opts.body,
42	      headers: { "content-type": "application/json", "x-correlation-id": cid, ...(opts.headers ?? {}) },
43	    });
44	  } catch (err) {
45	    // Loud failure, never silent (LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md §5).
46	    log("error", "api.network_error", { cid, method, url, error: String(err) });
47	    throw new ApiError(`Network error calling ${method} ${url}`, { cid, cause: err });
48	  }
49	
50	  if (!res.ok) {
51	    const body = await res.text().catch(() => "");
52	    log("error", "api.http_error", { cid, method, url, status: res.status, body: body.slice(0, 500) });
53	    throw new ApiError(`${method} ${url} failed: ${res.status}`, { cid, status: res.status, body });
54	  }
55	
56	  const data = (await res.json()) as T;
57	  log("info", "api.response", { cid, method, url, status: res.status });
58	  return data;
59	}

1	"""Chunked upload (B2, PR-2) — extracted from backend.main into its own router.
2	
3	Browser uploads arrive in sequential parts ≤ security.max_part_mb (free-tier edge
4	proxies cap request bodies at ~100 MB — HOSTING_PLAN §2), are assembled server-side
5	under security.upload_dir, then submitted to /api/process by the returned path.
6	Upload state is in-process by design (this is the single-box alpha): a server restart
7	aborts partial uploads and the client re-inits. Per-user partitioning of upload_dir
8	arrives with PR-3 identity scoping.
9	
10	The startup config lives on ``backend.main.global_config`` (set ONLY in ``main()``).
11	``_upload_cfg`` resolves it through the ``backend.main`` module object so it always
12	reads the live value the server set at startup (and so tests that monkeypatch
13	``backend.main.global_config`` flow through unchanged).
14	"""
15	import os
16	import threading
17	import uuid
18	from typing import Any, Dict, Optional
19	
20	from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException, Request
21	from pydantic import BaseModel
22	
23	from backend.utils.hashing import compute_video_id  # canonical file-identity hashing
24	from backend.utils.paths import safe_output_filename
25	
26	router = APIRouter()
27	
28	_uploads: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]] = {}
29	_uploads_lock = threading.Lock()
30	
31	
32	class UploadInitRequest(BaseModel):
33	    filename: str
34	    total_size_bytes: Optional[int] = None
35	
36	
37	class UploadCompleteRequest(BaseModel):
38	    total_parts: int
39	
40	
41	def _upload_cfg():
42	    # Resolved lazily (import inside the function) to read the LIVE startup config that
43	    # main() sets on the module global, and to avoid a circular import at module load
44	    # (main.py imports this router; this module must not import main at import time).
45	    import backend.main as _main
46	    sec = getattr(_main.global_config, "security", None)
47	    upload_dir = getattr(sec, "upload_dir", None) or "output/uploads"
48	    max_bytes = int(getattr(sec, "max_upload_mb", 4096) or 4096) * 1024 * 1024
49	    part_bytes = int(getattr(sec, "max_part_mb", 95) or 95) * 1024 * 1024
50	    exts = [e.lower().lstrip(".") for e in (getattr(sec, "allowed_upload_extensions", None) or ["mp4", "mov"])]
51	    return upload_dir, max_bytes, part_bytes, exts
52	
53	
54	def _abort_upload(upload_id: str) -> None:
55	    with _uploads_lock:
56	        st = _uploads.pop(upload_id, None)
57	    if st:
58	        try:
59	            os.remove(st["tmp_path"])
60	        except OSError:
61	            pass
62	
63	
64	@router.post("/api/upload/init")
65	def upload_init(req: UploadInitRequest):
66	    """Start a chunked upload. Returns upload_id; PUT sequential parts next."""
67	    upload_dir, max_bytes, part_bytes, exts = _upload_cfg()
68	    try:
69	        name = safe_output_filename(req.filename)
70	    except ValueError as e:
71	        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(e)) from e
72	    ext = os.path.splitext(name)[1].lower().lstrip(".")
73	    if ext not in exts:
74	        raise HTTPException(status_code=400,
75	                            detail=f"Extension '.{ext}' not allowed. Allowed: {sorted(exts)}")
76	    if req.total_size_bytes is not None and req.total_size_bytes > max_bytes:
77	        raise HTTPException(status_code=413,
78	                            detail=f"File exceeds the {max_bytes // (1024 * 1024)} MB upload limit.")
79	    os.makedirs(upload_dir, exist_ok=True)
80	    upload_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
81	    tmp_path = os.path.join(upload_dir, f".partial-{upload_id}")
82	    open(tmp_path, "wb").close()
83	    with _uploads_lock:
84	        _uploads[upload_id] = {"filename": name, "tmp_path": tmp_path, "parts": 0, "bytes": 0}
85	    return {"upload_id": upload_id, "max_part_mb": part_bytes // (1024 * 1024),
86	            "next_part": f"/api/upload/{upload_id}/part/0"}
87	
88	
89	@router.put("/api/upload/{upload_id}/part/{index}")
90	async def upload_part(upload_id: str, index: int, request:...

1	// Always-on VM host for the KhelSutra site — dependency-free (Node 22+ only).
2	// Serves public/ and POST /api/request-access into SQLite (node:sqlite), using the
3	// SAME handler logic as the Cloudflare Worker (src/index.js). This is the portability
4	// fallback "if Cloudflare acts out" — see docs/PORTABILITY.md.
5	//
6	//   PORT=8080 DB_PATH=/var/lib/khelsutra/access.db node server.js
7	//
8	import { createServer } from "node:http";
9	import { readFile } from "node:fs/promises";
10	import { join, extname, normalize } from "node:path";
11	import { DatabaseSync } from "node:sqlite";
12	import { handleRequest } from "./src/index.js";
13	import { sqliteStore } from "./src/store.js";
14	import { noopNotify } from "./src/notify.js";
15	
16	const PORT = Number(process.env.PORT) || 8080;
17	const ROOT = import.meta.dirname;
18	const PUBLIC = join(ROOT, "public");
19	const DB_PATH = process.env.DB_PATH || join(ROOT, "access.db");
20	
21	const db = new DatabaseSync(DB_PATH);
22	db.exec(await readFile(join(ROOT, "schema.sql"), "utf8")); // CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS — idempotent
23	const store = sqliteStore(db);
24	const notify = noopNotify(); // VM host: no email yet (add SMTP later — see docs/PORTABILITY.md)
25	
26	const TYPES = {
27	  ".html": "text/html; charset=utf-8", ".png": "image/png", ".svg": "image/svg+xml",
28	  ".css": "text/css", ".js": "text/javascript", ".ico": "image/x-icon", ".txt": "text/plain",
29	  ".mp4": "video/mp4", ".webm": "video/webm", ".gif": "image/gif", ".jpg": "image/jpeg", ".jpeg": "image/jpeg",
30	};
31	
32	async function toRequest(req) {
33	  const url = `http://${req.headers.host || "localhost"}${req.url}`;
34	  let body;
35	  if (req.method !== "GET" && req.method !== "HEAD") {
36	    const chunks = [];
37	    for await (const c of req) chunks.push(c);
38	    body = Buffer.concat(chunks);
39	  }
40	  return new Request(url, { method: req.method, headers: req.headers, body });
41	}
42	
43	async function send(res, response) {
44	  res.statusCode = response.status;
45	  for (const [k, v] of response.headers) res.setHeader(k, v);
46	  res.end(Buffer.from(await response.arrayBuffer()));
47	}
48	
49	async function serveStatic(pathname, res) {
50	  const rel = pathname === "/" ? "index.html"
51	    : pathname === "/capture" ? "capture.html"
52	    : pathname === "/own-model" ? "own-model.html"
53	    : pathname === "/flyer" ? "flyer.html"
54	    : pathname === "/flyer-kn" ? "flyer-kn.html"
55	    : pathname === "/flyer-hi" ? "flyer-hi.html"
56	    : pathname.replace(/^\/+/, "");
57	  const file = normalize(join(PUBLIC, rel));
58	  if (!file.startsWith(PUBLIC)) { res.statusCode = 403; return res.end("forbidden"); }
59	  try {
60	    const data = await readFile(file);
61	    res.setHeader("content-type", TYPES[extname(file)] || "application/octet-stream");
62	    res.end(data);
63	  } catch {
64	    res.statusCode = 404;
65	    res.end("not found");
66	  }
67	}
68	
69	createServer(async (req, res) => {
70	  try {
71	    const pathname = decodeURIComponent(new URL(`http://x${req.url}`).pathname);
72	    if (pathname === "/api/request-access") {
73	      if (req.method !== "POST") { res.statusCode = 405; return res.end('{"ok":false,"error":"method not allowed"}'); }
74	      return await send(res, await handleRequest(await toRequest(req), store, notify));
75	    }
76	    return await serveStatic(pathname, res);
77	  } catch (err) {
78	    res.statusCode = 500;
79	    res.end('{"ok":false,"error":"server error"}');
80	    console.error(err);
81	  }
82	}).listen(PORT, () => console.log(`khelsutra site listening on :${PORT}  (sqlite: ${DB_PATH})`));
83

## A4 — non-tech-savvy / phone-first UX (tracker `FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md` §7)

The Studio SPA was desktop-shaped: tiny tap targets and explanations hidden behind hover-only `title=` (a phone never shows `title=`). This makes it usable on a phone, for the Bangalore coach/parent audience.

### What changed
- **≥44px tap targets** (WCAG 2.5.5 / platform HIG) via Tailwind `min-h-11` / `h-11` on every primary control: the query input + Apply, the bulk buttons, the **sample-query chips**, the reel-name input + Build, the **language switcher**, the ingest input + submit + cancel + retry, and the compile **dismiss** (44px square) + **download** link.
- **Legibility:** the tiny parsed-pill + sample chips go `text-xs → text-sm`.
- **Tooltip-text → visible** (the A4 promise): the *"not available yet"* reason now renders as a visible line in the QueryBar (new `query.notAvailableHelp`, in en/kn/hi), and per-card **shot-count confidence** is shown inline on the RallyGrid chip instead of only in the hover title.

### Verification
- **128 web tests** green (new `a4.touch.test.tsx` pins the 44px floor + the visible help line + visible confidence); `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean; **key-parity** + **no-bare-strings** guards hold.
- **Browser-verified** at mobile (375px) and desktop: enlarged controls, the visible *"Shot type, players and score aren't available yet…"* line, and `~N shots · experimental — confidence: High` on the cards.
- **Reviewed by a 3-lens adversarial workflow** (touch-completeness / correctness / faithfulness): touch-completeness clean, correctness clean (re-ran the gate independently), and the one minor finding (per-card confidence was hover-only) is folded into this PR.

### Honesty notes
- kn/hi `notAvailableHelp` is AI-drafted (native review pending, consistent with the existing kn/hi catalogs).
- RallyGrid's `title=` is kept as redundant desktop hover detail; all essential info is now visible.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -3
[/external_agent_tool_call]

description: Create A5 PR
command: gh pr create --title "feat(web): A5 — friendly engine-error mapping across build + load (shared errors.*)" --base master --head feat/a5-friendly-build-errors --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## A5 — friendly engine-error mapping (tracker `FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md` §7)

The ingest flow already mapped failures to friendly, localized lines — but the **build** path and the **rally-load** path still surfaced raw client text (`ApiError: Network error calling POST /api/compile`, `HTTP 500`). For a non-technical coach/parent that's a dead end. This unifies all three SPA surfaces on one mapping.

### What changed
- **New `lib/errorText.ts`** — `errorText(t, e)`: an `engine` error shows the engine's **own** detail verbatim (the most specific, actionable message); every other code maps to a localized line in a **new shared `errors.*` namespace** (`engineGeneric` is the detail-less last resort). Kept out of the i18n-free `errors.ts` so that stays unit-testable.
- **`App.onBuild`** and the **rally-load** catch now go `classifyError → errorText` (localized), not `String(err)`/`parseDetail`. The `build.failed` / `rallies.load_failed` logs gain `code`+`detail` (observability) and keep `cid`+`status`.
- **`IngestPanel`** unified onto `errorText` (drops its inline `ingest.${code}` map).
- **Catalogs (en/kn/hi):** moved `ingest.{networkError,invalidUri,emptyResult,timeout}` → `errors.*`, added `errors.engineGeneric`, removed the now-unused `ingest.failed`.

### Verification
- **136 web tests** green: new `errorText.test.ts` (mapping + kn localization) and `build-error.test.tsx` (build network→"is it running?", 4xx→engine detail verbatim, **load**→friendly). `tsc` + `vite build` clean; **key-parity** + **no-bare-strings** hold.
- **Browser-verified**: with no engine on `:8000`, a submit shows *"Can't reach KhelSutra. Is it running?"* via the shared helper.
- **Reviewed by a 2-lens adversarial workflow** (regression/orphaned-keys + faithfulness/observability): no remaining `ingest.${code}` references, key sets identical across en/kn/hi, nothing dropped from the logs. The load-path fix in this PR folds the review's one "natural next consolidation" nit.

### Honesty notes
- kn/hi `errors.*` reuse the existing (AI-drafted) ingest translations + a new `engineGeneric` draft — native review still pending.
- A stored error string doesn't re-localize on a mid-error language switch (consistent with the existing build/mode handling); errors are transient.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -2
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## A8 — honest expectations + icon+text status (tracker `FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md` §7)

For a non-tech user on a slow uplink, a long silent wait reads as "broken". This adds **background-processing reassurance** and shows status as **icon + text chips (never icon-only)** across both the ingest progress and the build state.

### What changed
- **New `StatusChip`** — always icon **+** text (color is never the only signal, per the HCI4D guidance in §4) + a shared `Spinner`.
- **`IngestPanel`**: processing is now a *working* chip + a *"safe to wait — KhelSutra keeps working on the server"* reassurance line; a failure shows a **⚠ "Couldn't process"** chip above the friendly message.
- **`CompileResult`**: a new **building** state — a *"Stitching your reel…"* chip + reassurance, so the user isn't staring at a frozen footer; the **done / failed** headers gain a **✓ / ⚠** icon paired with the text, and the terminal card is `aria-live` so a screen reader announces the outcome.
- **Catalogs (en/kn/hi):** `+ingest.reassure`, `+ingest.statusFailed`, `+result.building`, `+result.buildingReassure` (hedged — no hard time promise).

### Verification
- **141 web tests** green (new `a8.status.test.tsx` covers building/done/error chips + ingest reassurance + the failure chip); `tsc` + `vite build` clean; **key-parity** + **no-bare-strings** hold.
- **Browser-verified**: the ingest failure chip renders live as **⚠ Couldn't process** + *"Can't reach KhelSutra. Is it running?"* + Try again.
- **Reviewed by a 2-lens adversarial workflow** (a11y/correctness + faithfulness); folded both findings — added `aria-live` to the terminal CompileResult card, and softened the build reassurance to a hedge (no hard "under a minute" claim).

### Honesty notes
- kn/hi strings are AI-drafted (native review pending).
- Screen-reader announcement of a freshly-mounted live region varies by SR; this is strictly better than the prior no-`aria-live` state. A fully stable always-mounted region is a possible later refinement.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -2

1	// @vitest-environment jsdom
2	//
3	// The ingest state machine: submit → poll (queued/running) → done | error. Drives the recursive
4	// setTimeout poll loop with fake timers so each tick is deterministic, and pins the load-bearing
5	// behaviours: the success-shaped-failure (done + no rallies → emptyResult, never a load), the
6	// two error surfaces (engine detail vs friendly network), and cancellation stopping the loop.
7	import { describe, it, expect, vi, afterEach } from "vitest";
8	import { renderHook, act } from "@testing-library/react";
9	import { useIngestJob } from "./useIngestJob";
10	import type { JobStatus } from "../api/types";
11	
12	type FetchFn = typeof fetch;
13	
14	function httpOk(status: number, json: unknown) {
15	  return { ok: status >= 200 && status < 300, status, json: async () => json, text: async () => JSON.stringify(json) };
16	}
17	
18	/** A fetch that answers POST /api/process with `process`, then walks `jobs` on each GET /api/jobs/*. */
19	function fetchSeq(process: unknown, jobs: JobStatus[]): FetchFn {
20	  let i = 0;
21	  return vi.fn(async (url: string | URL, init?: RequestInit) => {
22	    const u = String(url);
23	    const method = init?.method ?? "GET";
24	    if (u === "/api/process" && method === "POST") return process;
25	    if (u.startsWith("/api/jobs/")) {
26	      const body = jobs[Math.min(i, jobs.length - 1)];
27	      i += 1;
28	      return httpOk(200, body);
29	    }
30	    throw new Error(`unexpected fetch ${method} ${u}`);
31	  }) as unknown as FetchFn;
32	}
33	
34	/** Flush the submit microtasks, then walk the backoff ladder (1000 → 1500 → 2250 → …) for n ticks. */
35	async function drain(ticks: number) {
36	  await act(async () => {
37	    await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(0); // flush processVideo → schedule the first poll
38	  });
39	  let delay = 1000;
40	  for (let k = 0; k < ticks; k += 1) {
41	    await act(async () => {
42	      await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(delay);
43	    });
44	    delay = Math.min(delay * 1.5, 5000);
45	  }
46	}
47	
48	afterEach(() => {
49	  vi.restoreAllMocks();
50	  vi.unstubAllGlobals();
51	  vi.useRealTimers();
52	});

+// ── chunked upload (backend/api/uploads.py) ──────────────────────────────────
+// A browser uploads a LOCAL file in sequential parts ≤ max_part_mb (free-tier edge proxies cap

+/** Start a chunked upload. 400 = bad extension; 413 = over the size limit [uploads.py:64]. */

+  log("info", "api.request", { cid: c, method: "PUT", url });
+  let res: Response;
+  try {
+    res = await fetch(url, {
+      method: "PUT",
+      body: chunk,
+      headers: { "content-type": "application/octet-stream", "x-correlation-id": c },
+      signal,
+    });
+  } catch (err) {
+    log("error", "api.network_error", { cid: c, method: "PUT", url, error: String(err) });
+    throw new ApiError(`Network error calling PUT ${url}`, { cid: c, cause: err });
+  }
+  if (!res.ok) {
+    const body = await res.text().catch(() => "");

+  }
+  log("info", "api.response", { cid: c, method: "PUT", url, status: res.status });
+  return (await res.json()) as UploadPartResponse;
+}

+    method: "POST",
+    body: JSON.stringify({ total_parts: totalParts }),
+    cid,
+  });
+}

command: gh pr create --title "feat(web): T4 — in-UI file upload (the runnable-tool keystone)" --base master --head feat/t4-in-ui-upload --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## T4 — in-UI local file upload (North Star keystone · `LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md` §9)

Alpha users have a **file**, not a `gs://` URI. The ingest screen now lets them upload a local video straight from the browser, wired to the engine's **existing** chunked-upload endpoints (`backend/api/uploads.py`) and converging on the **same** process → poll → load → compile → download back-half. This is the piece that makes the tool point-and-go for a non-technical user.

### What changed (100% `web/`, zero engine code)
- **`api/client.ts`** — `uploadInit` / `uploadPart` (raw octet-stream PUT) / `uploadComplete` / `uploadAbort` + a **`uploadFile()`** orchestrator: slices the file into sequential parts ≤ the engine's `max_part_mb`, reports byte progress, is `AbortController`-cancellable, and best-effort `DELETE`s the server-side partial on any failure/abort. The returned server **path** feeds straight into `POST /api/process`.
- **`hooks/useUpload.ts`** — `idle → uploading{pct} → hand the path to onComplete | error`; rejects a 0-byte file before any request; `classifyError` on failures.
- **`IngestPanel`** — a **file picker** (primary; keyboard-focusable, **focus-visible**, ≥44px) + the existing `gs://` URI form (secondary); a determinate **% progressbar** (named, with a sighted-only %); reuses the A8 status chips + the A5 `errorText` mapping; one Cancel / Retry for either half.
- **Catalogs (en/kn/hi)** — `+ingest.chooseFile / uploadAria / fileHint / orLink / uploadingStatus / uploadProgress`.

### Verification
- **153 web tests** green: `api/upload.test.ts` (chunk math, strict 0,1,2 order, abort cleanup, init-reject), `hooks/useUpload.test.ts` (empty-file guard, happy path → `onComplete`, classified error), `components/upload.test.tsx` (pick → upload → `/api/process` gets the **server path** → `onLoaded` with the **job's canonical** video_id; focus-visible). `tsc` + `vite build` clean; key-parity + no-bare-strings hold.
- **Browser-verified**: the labelled file picker + "OR USE A CLOUD LINK" both render; picking a file triggers the real upload (`/api/upload/init`), surfacing the friendly **⚠ Couldn't process** chip when no engine is up — exactly as expected.
- **Reviewed by a 3-lens adversarial workflow** (correctness/chunking · a11y/UX · engine-contract). Contract **verified clean** against `uploads.py`; folded the focus-visible (WCAG 2.4.7), NaN-safe `max_part_mb`, and named-progressbar findings.
- Rebased onto `origin/master` (after #64/#65); `apiBase()`/`VITE_API_BASE` and the upload helpers coexist (the upload calls use `${BASE}`).

### Deployment note (for T2, not this PR)
In **hosted mode** with edge-auth ON, the engine's `allowed_video_root` must contain `upload_dir` or `/api/process` will reject uploaded paths. Default single-user mode (the alpha target) needs no change.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -2

This is a **parallel** line: it adds locales, it does **not** re-architect i18n.
7	> **Honesty note:** each claim tagged `[verified]` (checked vs the working tree this session), `[researched]` (sourced, needs sign-off), or `[design]` (proposed). Not legal advice — the OFL-font carve-out is the owner's standing call (D7 in FRIENDLY).

10	
11	## 0. TL;DR
12	Add **Thai, Indonesian, Danish, Spanish, Chinese (Simplified), Japanese, Korean** to the Studio SPA with the **same rigor as en/kn/hi**. The good news `[verified]`: the i18n foundation already scales — **zero changes to the negotiation contract or the catalog format**; adding a locale is "drop a reviewed `common.json` + (if non-Latin) bundle one OFL Noto font + keep CI key-parity green." The work splits cleanly into **Phase A** (es/da/id — *catalog-only, no fonts*), **Phase B** (Thai — *one small OFL font*), and **Phase C** (zh-Hans/ja/ko — *large OFL CJK fonts + lazy-load + an offline-bundle subsetting decision + the zh script-variant call*). **The single most important caveats:** (1) **native-speaker review per language** is the real cost and the public-launch gate (Gemini drafts are *inference-only*, never the source of truth — the standing guardrail); (2) **CJK fonts add tens of MB** to the otherwise-tiny air-gapped bundle; (3) **Spanish now has a 3-form plural** (`{one, many, other}`) — the one authoring wrinkle.
13	
14	## 1. Problem & goal
15	**Why now.** en/kn/hi shipped and proved the foundation is locale-pluggable (a Kannada-default browser auto-renders Kannada; the switcher walks kn→hi→en). The owner wants to broaden reach beyond the India beachhead — **SE Asia (th, id), Europe (da, es), and East Asia (zh, ja, ko)**.
16	**Concrete outcome ("good"):** a user whose browser is in any of the 7 languages lands on the Studio (and, once `site/` is localized via FRIENDLY B3, the marketing pages too), and — without help — understands the product, runs the tool, and gets a reel, **entirely in their language**, with the fonts rendering crisply offline.
17	**Read to get current:** this doc §3–§4; [`FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md`](FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md) (the foundation this rides on, esp. §4 design + §6.5 negotiation tests); `web/src/i18n/index.ts` (the detector/ICU/fallback config) + `web/src/main.tsx` (the `@fontsource` font-load pattern) + `web/src/i18n/hygiene.test.ts` (key-parity + no-bare-strings + offline guards).

## MULTILINGUAL Phase A — es/da/id (`MULTILINGUAL_EXPANSION.md` §7, owner-approved #58)

Adds **Spanish, Danish, Indonesian** to the Studio SPA — Latin-script, **catalog-only, no new fonts**, same rigor as en/kn/hi. The foundation scales unchanged: add to `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` + a `common.json`.

### What changed
- `web/src/i18n/locales/{es,da,id}/common.json` (AI-drafted → **native review pending**) + `lang.{es,da,id}` autonyms in **every** catalog (key-parity). Wired into `i18n/index.ts` resources + `LanguageSwitcher`.
- **Plurals per CLDR:** **`es` is 3-form `{one, many, other}`** (the one authoring wrinkle), `da` is 2-form `{one, other}`, `id` is single-form `{other}`.
- **Tests (coverage up):** es/da/id render + auto-negotiation (`es-ES`/`da-DK`/`id-ID` → base) + es/id plural word-forms; a hygiene check that the es plural messages carry `one`/`many`/`other` arms.

### Bonus guard — caught a real bug
Added a **placeholder-parity** guard (every locale's value must keep en's `{placeholder}` set — something key-parity doesn't check). It immediately flagged a **pre-existing bug**: `kn`/`hi` `mode.loading` + `mode.error` still carried the `{videoId}` placeholder that **A1 (#60) removed** from the en copy. Re-drafted the stale kn/hi `mode.*` + `build.noVideo` to match A1's friendly copy.

### Verification
- **168 web tests** green; `tsc` + `vite build` clean; key-parity + no-bare-strings + offline guards hold.
- **Browser-verified:** es renders ("Elige las jugadas para tu reel", footer "6 jugadas seleccionadas" — 3-form plural), da renders ("Vælg duellerne til din reel", "6 dueller valgt"), the switcher lists all **6 languages**, `<html lang>` syncs.

### Scope
Phase **B** (Thai — needs one OFL font) and **C** (zh-Hans/ja/ko — large CJK fonts + offline-subset) stay **owner-gated** (`MULTILINGUAL_EXPANSION.md` §8 Q2/Q3/Q4). All AI drafts await native-speaker review before wide public launch.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -2
[/external_agent_tool_call]

--- /dev/null
+++ b/web/src/components/TapFilters.tsx
@@ -0,0 +1,58 @@
+// Localized tap-only filter controls (tracker A9). The query bar PARSES ENGLISH text (a documented
+// limit) — so a Kannada/Hindi/… user who can't type English needs to filter by TAPPING. Each control
+// builds the SAME ParsedQuery the parser would for its English equivalent — directly, no English
+// round-trip — and applies it through the shared onApply path. A test pins tap == type so a control
+// can never silently drift from the parser. Length-led + fully-available only (never shot-count).
+import { useTranslation } from "react-i18next";
+import type { ParsedQuery } from "../lib/query";
+
+interface TapFilter {
+  /** i18n label key (localized) — the user sees this, not the English query. */
+  key: string;
+  /** The equivalent typed query; the tap MUST select identically to typing this (pinned by a test). */
+  english: string;
+  parts: Pick<ParsedQuery, "filters" | "sort" | "limit">;
+}
+
+export const TAP_FILTERS: TapFilter[] = [
+  { key: "tap.longest", english: "longest first", parts: { filters: [], sort: { key: "duration", dir: "desc" } } },
+  { key: "tap.shortest", english: "shortest first", parts: { filters: [], sort: { key: "duration", dir: "asc" } } },
+  { key: "tap.over10", english: "over 10s", parts: { filters: [{ field: "duration", op: ">", value: 10 }] } },
+  { key: "tap.over20", english: "over 20s", parts: { filters: [{ field: "duration", op: ">", value: 20 }] } },
+  { key: "tap.top5", english: "top 5", parts: { filters: [], sort: { key: "duration", dir: "desc" }, limit: 5 } },
+  { key: "tap.top10", english: "top 10", parts: { filters: [], sort: { key: "duration", dir: "desc" }, limit: 10 } },
+];
+
+interface Props {
+  /** Same handler the QueryBar uses — App runs runQuery + writes the shared selection. */
+  onApply: (q: ParsedQuery, source: "typed" | "example") => void;
+}
+
+export function TapFilters({ onApply }: Props) {
+  const { t } = useTranslation();
+  // ≥44px tap targets (A4) so the no-type path is comfortable on a phone.
+  const BTN = "inline-flex min-h-11 items-center rounded-full border border-black/15 px-4 text-sm hover:border-green";
+
+  return (
+    <div className="mt-3" data-testid="tap-filters">
+      <span className="text-xs text-ink/40">{t("tap.title")}</span>
+      <div className="mt-1.5 flex flex-wrap gap-2">
+        {TAP_FILTERS.map((f) => (
+          <button
+            key={f.key}
+            type="button"
+            className={BTN}
+            onClick={() =>
+              onApply(
+                { raw: `tap:${f.key}`, filters: f.parts.filters, sort: f.parts.sort, limit: f.parts.limit, unavailable: [], recognized: true },
+                "example",
+              )
+            }
+          >
+            {t(f.key)}
+          </button>
+        ))}
+      </div>
+    </div>
+  );
+}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## A9 — tap-only localized filters (`FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md` §6.5 / §7)

The query bar **parses English** (a documented limit). A Kannada/Hindi/… user who can't type English had no way to filter without help. A9 adds **localized tap controls** alongside the bar — the §6.5 bridge until NL parsing exists.

### What changed
- **New `TapFilters`** — localized **Sort** (longest/shortest) + **Length** (over 10s/20s) + **Count** (top 5/10) controls (≥44px, A4). Each builds the **same `ParsedQuery` the parser would** for its English equivalent — directly, no English round-trip — and routes through the shared `onApply` path. A test pins **tap == type** for *every* control (the emitted query deep-equals `parseQuery(english)`).
- **`QueryBar`**: the English example chips now render **only in English** — for a non-English user they were an untappable wall stacked above the localized tap row (the review's main finding).
- **Catalogs (en/kn/hi/es/da/id)**: a `tap.*` block (self-contained "Tap to filter:" + 6 control labels).

### Verification
- **173 web tests** green: `TapFilters.test.tsx` (tap==type table + **every-control emit** check + localized labels + ≥44px); `tsc` + `vite build` clean; key-parity + no-bare-strings + the new placeholder-parity guard all hold.
- **Browser-verified**: en shows the type-examples **and** the tap controls; **kn shows only the localized tap controls** (English row hidden) with a self-contained title; tapping "Over 10 seconds" filters to 3 rallies (tap == type).
- **Reviewed by a 2-lens adversarial workflow** — correctness **clean** (tap==type traced for all 6). Folded all UX/nit findings: the non-en declutter, the self-contained "Tap to filter:" copy, logging `raw` as the English equivalent (cleaner trace), and looping the emit-equals-parser test over every control.

### Honesty notes
- kn/hi/es/da/id `tap.*` are AI-drafted (native review pending).
- The free-text query bar stays English-grammar; A9 is the tap bridge, not NL parsing.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -2

### 869. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.530Z)

Verified against the schema and the API — answer is **no**.

The site's rendered locale is **purely a browser-side concern** and never reaches the engine or the database:

**What actually gets stored when a video is processed:**
- `videos` table ([database.py:118](backend/infrastructure/database.py:118)): `id, filepath, processed_at, status, validation_results` (+ `user_id` from the v4 migration). No locale.
- `jobs` table ([database.py:193](backend/infrastructure/database.py:193), one row per `/api/process`): `id, video_path, video_id, segmenter, player_pool, max_frames, status, progress, error, result, created_at/started_at/finished_at` (+ `policy`, `next_poll_at`, and the v6 cost columns). No locale.
- `play_segments` / `events` / `candidate_segments` — no locale either.

**Where the locale lives instead:** only in the browser — the i18n detector persists the choice to `localStorage["khelsutra.lang"]` ([web/src/i18n/index.ts:24](web/src/i18n/index.ts:24)). The SPA's outgoing calls don't carry it: `processVideo` sends only `{video_path}` and `uploadInit` sends only `{filename, total_size_bytes}` ([web/src/api/client.ts](web/src/api/client.ts)). The engine does no `Accept-Language` handling. (The only `language` hit in the backend is the `obsreport` config-DSL in `reporting/spec.yaml` — unrelated, and the dead `backend/api/static/`.)

So today a Kannada-rendered session and an English-rendered session that process the same file produce identical DB rows.

**Whether that's a gap depends on what you want it for** — and there are two real reasons it might matter:
1. **Localized output** — a Kannada/Hindi **reel watermark** or a localized report. The engine's `WatermarkConfig.font_path` already takes a path (FRIENDLY §4 flagged this), but the engine would need to *know* the locale at process time to pick the language/font. Recording it is the natural way.
2. **Analytics** — which language markets are actually using the tool (a PMF signal).

If you want it, it's a small, clean, additive change: add an optional `locale` to the `/api/process` (and upload-init) request, have the SPA pass `i18n.resolvedLanguage`, and store it as a nullable column on `videos` (or `jobs`) via a v7 migration — additive-NULL, default-OFF behavior, no impact on existing rows.

Want me to spec/build that (it'd touch the engine, so I'd coordinate with the concurrent serving session and likely fold it into the same v7 migration they're planning), or were you just checking the current state?

Here's the spec, then I'll build it.

## Spec — locale capture + localized watermarks

**Architecture (deliberately decoupled, to ship the watermark without waiting on a DB migration):**

| Concern | How | Repo | Migration? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Localized watermark** | `CompileRequest.locale` → a `localize_watermark(cfg, locale)` helper overrides `text` + `font_path` per locale → ffmpeg `drawtext`. en/es/da/id use RobotoSlab; kn/hi use bundled Noto. | engine | **No** |
| **SPA sends locale** | pass `i18n.resolvedLanguage` to `/api/compile` (watermark) **and** `/api/process` (analytics) | khelsutra | — |
| **PMF analytics** | `ProcessRequest.locale` → `jobs.locale` column → `SELECT locale, COUNT(*) FROM jobs GROUP BY locale` | engine | **v7 (the collision-risk piece)** |

**3 PRs, lowest-risk first:** PR-1 watermark (engine, no migration) → PR-2 SPA (no engine collision) → PR-3 analytics v7 migration (engine, **done last + in an isolated worktree + re-verified against `origin/master`** so it can't collide with the serving session's planned v7 — renumber to v8 if theirs lands first). Sending `locale` to `/api/process` before PR-3 is harmless (Pydantic ignores unknown fields).

**Watermark fallback (honest):** unknown/missing locale → the existing default text+font (en behavior unchanged). If a Noto font is somehow absent, the compiler already retries without the watermark rather than failing the reel.

Let me build. Setting up tasks + an isolated engine worktree for PR-1, and fetching the fonts:

[/external_agent_tool_call]

# Bundled fonts

Fonts shipped with the highlight **watermark** (`stitching.watermark`). All bundled fonts
are **Apache-2.0** — commercial-clean per the project licensing guardrail (MIT/Apache/BSD
only), so they can be redistributed with the tool and self-hosted derivatives.

| File | Font | License | Source |
|------|------|---------|--------|
| `RobotoSlab-Regular.ttf` | Roboto Slab | Apache-2.0 (see `LICENSE-Apache-2.0.txt`) | https://github.com/google/fonts/tree/main/apache/robotoslab |

`RobotoSlab-Regular.ttf` is the **default** watermark font: `stitching.watermark.font_path`
defaults to it so the overlay renders **without a system fontconfig setup** (a common gap
on Windows, where `font='Sans'` fails). To use a different typeface — your own, or another
bundled one — set `stitching.watermark.font_path` to its path in `config.json`.

To add more bundled options, drop the `.ttf` here, keep it **MIT/Apache/BSD-licensed**, and
add a row to the table above (with its license file).

retries the encode without the watermark if drawtext can't render — a font gap never nukes the reel.

## Localized reel watermark

The reel's branding watermark now renders in the language the SPA was used in — a Kannada/Hindi/Spanish/… user's highlight reel is watermarked in their language, with a script-appropriate font.

### What changed
- **New `backend/pipeline/stitcher/watermark_i18n.py`** — `localize_watermark(cfg, locale)` overrides the watermark `text` (and a script `font_path` for non-Latin) per locale: en/es/da/id keep RobotoSlab; **kn → Noto Sans Kannada, hi → Noto Sans Devanagari**. The brand token "KhelSutra.guru" stays Latin. **Unknown/missing/`en` locale → the configured default UNCHANGED** (no behavior change for existing reels).
- **`CompileRequest` gains an optional `locale`**; `/api/compile` localizes `stitching.watermark` before compiling. Backward-compatible — absent locale = today's behavior.
- **Bundled Noto Sans Kannada + Devanagari (OFL-1.1)** in `backend/assets/fonts/` — the **D7 OFL-for-media carve-out** (the same faces the SPA self-hosts) + `LICENSE-OFL-1.1.txt` + README attribution.

### Graceful by construction
A missing script font falls back to RobotoSlab (no crash), and the compiler already retries the encode **without** the watermark if `drawtext` can't render — a font gap can never nuke the reel.

### Verification
- **Full suite: 1302 passed / 7 skipped**; `ruff` + `mypy` clean (rebased onto latest `master`, re-verified — 75 targeted tests incl. the new app-launcher tests green).
- New `tests/test_watermark_i18n.py`: pass-through (en/unknown/None), Latin text-only override, kn/hi text + bundled Noto font, BCP-47 base-subtag normalization, no input mutation, fonts ship + valid sfnt + OFL, and a localized watermark flowing into the `drawtext` filtergraph.
- **Rendered with real ffmpeg** — Kannada (`KhelSutra.guru ನಿಂದ ರಚಿಸಲಾಗಿದೆ`) and Devanagari (`KhelSutra.guru से बनाया गया`) draw correctly (no tofu), Latin brand intact.

### Notes
- Localized watermark strings are AI-drafted → **native-speaker review pending** (same posture as the SPA catalogs).
- The SPA must pass `locale` to `/api/compile` to activate this (separate khelsutra PR); this is inert + backward-compatible until then.
- Built in an isolated worktree off `origin/master`; the concurrent serving session's working tree was untouched.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF

20	export function IngestPanel({ onLoaded }: Props) {
21	  const { t } = useTranslation();
22	  const [uri, setUri] = useState("");
23	  const { phase, submit, cancel: cancelIngest, reset: resetIngest, busy: ingestBusy } = useIngestJob(onLoaded);
24	  // On upload completion, feed the returned server PATH straight into the engine processing back-half.
25	  const upload = useUpload((path) => submit(path));
26	  const busy = ingestBusy || upload.busy;
27	
28	  const onSubmitUri = (e: FormEvent) => {
29	    e.preventDefault();
30	    submit(uri);
31	  };
32	  const onFile = (e: ChangeEvent<HTMLInputElement>) => {
33	    const file = e.target.files?.[0];
34	    if (file) upload.start(file);
35	    e.target.value = ""; // let re-picking the same file fire change again
36	  };
37	  const cancelAll = () => {
38	    upload.cancel();
39	    cancelIngest();
40	  };
41	  const resetAll = () => {
42	    upload.reset();
43	    resetIngest();
44	  };

## LW2 — record the UI locale on the engine calls that need it

Pairs with the engine watermark localization. The SPA now sends the language it was rendered in on the two calls that use it:

- **`/api/compile`** → localizes the **reel branding watermark** (engine LW1).
- **`/api/process`** → records the locale for **PMF analytics** (which markets use the tool; engine LW3).

### What changed
- `compileReel` + `processVideo` (`api/client.ts`) take an optional `locale`, included in the body and **omitted when absent** (`JSON.stringify` drops `undefined` → full back-compat).
- `IngestPanel` passes `i18n.resolvedLanguage` on **both** ingest paths (URI + file upload), threaded through `useIngestJob.submit(uri, locale)` → `processVideo`.
- `App.onBuild` passes `i18n.resolvedLanguage` to `compileReel`.

### Verification
- **177 web tests** green (`api/locale.test.ts`: locale included when present / omitted when absent on both calls; a `build-error.test` App-integration check that the active locale reaches `/api/compile`); `tsc` + `vite build` clean.
- No visible UI change (data passthrough) — fully test-covered.
- **Inert + backward-compatible** until the engine PRs land: the engine ignores an unknown `/api/process` field (Pydantic) and falls back to the default watermark for an absent/unknown compile locale.

Engine side: watermark = [Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer#304](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/304) (LW1); analytics migration = LW3 (next).

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -2

64	    def _add_column_idempotent(cursor: sqlite3.Cursor, ddl: str) -> None:
65	        """Apply a bare ``ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN`` migration step re-runnably.
66	
67	        A DDL ``ALTER`` auto-commits independently of the surrounding ``with conn:``
68	        transaction, so an interrupted v3/v4 block can leave the column added while
69	        ``user_version`` stays un-bumped — and the next open would re-run the same
70	        ``ALTER`` and raise ``OperationalError: duplicate column name``, bricking every
71	        future ``Database(...)`` construction (``_init_db`` runs in ``__init__``). Swallow
72	        ONLY that specific error so the ladder stays crash-safe, matching the re-runnable
73	        ``CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS`` v1/v2/v5 blocks.
74	        """
75	        try:
76	            cursor.execute(ddl)
77	        except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
78	            if "duplicate column name" not in str(exc).lower():
79	                raise

292	    def _migrate_to_v6(self, cursor):
293	        # v6: per-job COST LEDGER (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M8, D8). NULLABLE cost columns on
294	        # `jobs` (NULL = no cost recorded = byte-identical to today; nothing records until
295	        # billing.enabled + a real pricebook) + a VERSIONED `pricebook` table. Cost is stored in
296	        # USD (matches GCP billing = one source of truth); INR is a display conversion at a
297	        # configurable FX rate. ALTER (not recreate) so v1-v5 job rows survive; ADD COLUMNs are
298	        # idempotent so an interrupted upgrade can re-run them. Additive ONLY — no served change.
299	        for col, typ in (
300	            ("owner_id", "TEXT"),               # who pays (the non-owner user / tenant)
301	            ("compute_backend", "TEXT"),        # local_gpu | cloud_run | ...
302	            ("gpu_type", "TEXT"),               # L4 | T4 | ... (selects the per-second GPU rate)
303	            ("gpu_active_seconds", "REAL"),
304	            ("wall_seconds", "REAL"),
305	            ("bytes_out", "INTEGER"),           # egress
306	            ("gemini_cost_usd", "REAL"),        # provider-reported, already USD
307	            ("pricebook_version", "TEXT"),
308	            ("cost_usd", "REAL"),               # computed total (USD = source of truth)
309	            ("cost_breakdown_json", "TEXT"),    # {gpu_seconds: .., egress_gb: .., gemini_usd: ..}
310	        ):
311	            self._add_column_idempotent(cursor, f"ALTER TABLE jobs ADD COLUMN {col} {typ}")
312	        # Partial index keeps the NULL=no-cost fast path churn-free while making per-owner billing cheap.
313	        cursor.execute("CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_jobs_owner ON jobs(owner_id) "
314	                       "WHERE owner_id IS NOT NULL")
315	        # Versioned pricebook: one row per (version, resource, gpu_type). Rates change + differ by
316	        # gpu_type → re-version (never mutate a shipped version, so a recorded cost stays auditable).
317	        cursor.execute("""
318	            CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pricebook (
319	                version TEXT NOT NULL,
320	                resource TEXT NOT NULL,
321	                gpu_type TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
322	                unit_price_usd REAL NOT NULL DEFAULT 0.0,
323	                currency TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'USD',
324	                effective_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
325	            )
326	        """)
327	        cursor.execute("CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_pricebook_key "
328	                       "ON pricebook(version, resource, gpu_type)")
329	        # Seed a PLACEHOLDER version with every rate 0.0 → cost_usd computes to 0 (nothing
330	        # user-facing changes). The owner INSERTs a real, calibrated version (real GCP $/GPU-s,
331	        # egress $/GB) and flips billing.pricebook_version to it — never overwrite this seed.
332	        for resource, gpu_type in (("gpu_seconds", "L4"), ("gpu_seconds", "T4"),
333	                                   ("wall_seconds", ""), ("egress_gb", "")):
334	            cursor.execute(
335	                "INSERT OR IGNORE INTO pricebook (version, resource, gpu_type, unit_price_usd, "
336	                "currency) VALUES ('placeholder-v0', ?, ?, 0.0, 'USD')", (resource, gpu_type))
337	        cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 6")

def test_v6_migration_adds_cost_columns_and_seeds_placeholder(tmp_path):
    db = Database(str(tmp_path / "m.db"))
    with db._connect() as conn:
        ver = conn.execute("PRAGMA user_version").fetchone()[0]
        cols = {r[1] for r in conn.execute("PRAGMA table_info(jobs)").fetchall()}
        seeded = conn.execute(
            "SELECT unit_price_usd FROM pricebook WHERE version='placeholder-v0'").fetchall()
    assert ver >= 6
    assert {"cost_usd", "gpu_active_seconds", "wall_seconds", "bytes_out", "owner_id",
            "pricebook_version", "cost_breakdown_json", "compute_backend", "gpu_type"} <= cols
    assert len(seeded) >= 4 and all(r[0] == 0.0 for r in seeded)  # placeholder = every rate $0

213	def test_migration_v3_db_upgrades_to_v6_preserving_rows(tmp_path):
214	    """A real v3 DB (jobs + candidates already populated) upgrades cleanly to v6: the new
215	    columns/tables appear, user_id back-fills to NULL, and prior rows survive the ALTERs."""
216	    db_path = tmp_path / "legacy_v3.db"
217	    db = Database(str(db_path))  # fresh DB is born at the current version; simulate a v3 state
218	    # Seed pre-upgrade data, then force the version back to 3 and re-open to run v4+v5.
219	    db.add_video("vidA", "a.mp4", "processed", [{"v": 1}])
220	    db.add_candidate_segment("vidA", "wasb_hybrid", 2.0, 9.0, stats={"net_crossings": 3})
221	    conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path))
222	    conn.execute("DROP INDEX IF EXISTS idx_videos_user")
223	    conn.execute("DROP INDEX IF EXISTS idx_candidates_user")
224	    conn.execute("DROP TABLE IF EXISTS user_models")
225	    # Recreate v3-shaped tables WITHOUT user_id by rebuilding (simplest: just rewind version
226	    # and drop the v4/v5 artifacts that a true v3 DB wouldn't have).
227	    conn.execute("ALTER TABLE videos DROP COLUMN user_id")
228	    conn.execute("ALTER TABLE candidate_segments DROP COLUMN user_id")
229	    conn.execute("PRAGMA user_version = 3")
230	    conn.commit()
231	    conn.close()
232	    assert "user_id" not in _table_cols(db_path, "videos")
233	
234	    # Re-open: the migration ladder must carry the v3 DB up to v6 without data loss.
235	    db2 = Database(str(db_path))
236	    conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path))
237	    assert conn.execute("PRAGMA user_version").fetchone()[0] == 6
238	    tables = {r[0] for r in conn.execute(
239	        "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table'").fetchall()}
240	    assert "user_models" in tables       # v5
241	    assert "pricebook" in tables          # v6 (cost ledger)
242	    conn.close()
243	    assert "user_id" in _table_cols(db_path, "videos")
244	    assert "cost_usd" in _table_cols(db_path, "jobs")  # v6 cost columns back-filled (NULL)
245	    assert "user_id" in _table_cols(db_path, "candidate_segments")

## v7 — per-job UI locale (PMF analytics)

Captures the language the SPA was used in on every `/api/process` submission, so we can answer *"which language markets actually use the tool?"* — and it complements the localized reel watermark ([#304](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/304)).

### What changed
- **v7 migration**: a **NULLABLE `locale` column on `jobs`** (`NULL` = unrecorded = byte-identical to today: CLI / older clients / pre-v7 rows). Additive-only `ALTER`, idempotent (re-runnable, crash-safe).
- **`ProcessRequest` gains an optional `locale`**; `/api/process` stores it via `create_job(locale=…)`.
- **New `Database.count_jobs_by_locale()`** → `{locale: count}` (busiest-first; `NULL` → `'(unset)'`) — the analytics primitive.

### Migration coordination
Authored in an **isolated worktree off `origin/master`**; re-verified there is **no competing v7** before committing and again after rebasing onto the latest master (the serving session's planned consent-cols migration can take **v8** — this is a small, self-contained, easily-renumbered ladder step). The concurrent session's working tree was untouched.

### Verification
- **Full suite: 1315 passed / 7 skipped**; `ruff` + `mypy` clean.
- New `tests/test_locale_analytics.py`: v7 fresh DB, a **v6→v7 in-place upgrade** that back-fills `locale=NULL` on prior rows, `create_job` storage, the `count_jobs_by_locale` aggregation (incl. the `(unset)` bucket), and `ProcessRequest.locale`. Plus an `/api/process` locale-capture test in `test_api_endpoints.py`.
- Bumped the schema-version contract assertions **6 → 7** across `test_async_jobs` / `test_database` / `test_job_worker_loop` (the intentional-drift guard).

### Notes
- The SPA already sends `locale` to `/api/process` (khelsutra #73), and Pydantic ignored it until this PR — so this activates the capture with **zero** client change needed.
- Analytics is queryable now (`count_jobs_by_locale` / SQL `GROUP BY locale`); a read-only stats endpoint can come later if wanted.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
